Eccles is the Distinguished Professor of Education at the University of California, Irvine and formerly the McKeachie/Pintrich Distinguished University Professor of Psychology and Education at the University of Michigan, as well as Senior Research Scientist and Director of the Gender and Achievement Research Program at the Institute for Social Research at the University of Michigan.  \n  \nOver the past 30 years, Professor Eccles has conducted research on a wide variety of topics including gender-role socialization, teacher expectancies, classroom influences on student motivation, and social development in the family and school context."}

Events\n\n# Emeritus faculty member Dr. Jacquelynne Eccles selected to receive 2017 APA Distinguished Scientific Contribution Award \n\n1. News and Events\n2. \n3. News\n4. 1. Alumni News\n    2. Faculty News\n    3. Graduate News\n    4. Staff News\n    5. Undergraduate News\n    6. Awards\n    7. Department Newsletters\n    8. Search News\n    9. Archived News\n5. Events\n\nDr.\nJacquelynne Eccles, Wilbert McKeachie and Paul Pintrich Distinguished University Professor Emerita of Psychology and Education; Professor Emerita of Women's Studies; Research Professor Emerita, RCGD, was selected to receive the 2017 APA Distinguished Scientific Contribution Award.\n\nThis award \"honors psychologists who have made distinguished theoretical or empirical contributions to basic research in psychology.\" A ceremony and reception honoring all 2017 APA award recipients will take place at the APA convention in Washington, DC on Friday, August 4, 2017.\n\nCongratulations to Dr. Eccles!\n\n[Tweet](https://twitter.com/share) [Email](mailto:?body=https://lsa.umich.edu/psych/news-events/all-news/awa"}

Eccles\" site:umich.edu", "url": "https://naeducation.org/member/jacquelynne-eccles/", "title": "Jacquelynne Eccles - National Academy of Education", "snippet": "# Jacquelynne Eccles\n\nUniversity of California, Irvine\n\nDistinguished Professor of Education\n\njacquelynne_eccles\n\n[Website](http://www.faculty.uci.edu/profile.cfm?faculty_id=5971)\n\n**Year Elected**\n\n1998\n\n**Membership status**\n\nEmeritus\n\nJacquelynne Eccles is Wilbert McKeachie Collegiate Professor of Psychology, at the University of Michigan. She received her BA from the University of California, Berkeley, and her PhD from the University of California, Los Angeles. Eccles has received the APS James McKeen Cattell Award, Society for Research on Adolescence Hill Award from the, the Div. 15 APAThorndyke Award, and the Div. 9 APA Kurt Lewin Award. She is past president of the Society for Research on Adolescence and one of the founders of the AERA Motivation SIG. Among other publications, she is co-author or co-editor of Managing to Make It, Gender and Occupational Outcomes, and Unexpected Educational Pathways."}

Eccles\" site:umich.edu", "url": "http://learningandtheadolescentmind.org/people_02.html", "title": "Jacquelynne Eccles", "snippet": "Learning and the Adolescent Mind\n\n* Home\n* Ideas\n* People\n* Practices\n* Resources\n\n|Robert Balfanz\n\nAlbert Bandura\n\nCarol Dweck\n\nJacquelynne Eccles\n\nPhilip Uri Treisman\n\nBarry Zimmerman |Jacquelynne Eccles\n\nJacquelynne Eccles\n\nJacquelynne Eccles\n\nJacquelynne Eccles is a professor of psychology and a research scientist at the University of Michigan. For over 30 years, she has conducted research on adolescent and student development, including classroom influences on student motivation, teachers\u2019 expectations, the social development of students, and gender-role socialization.\n\nProfessor Eccles\u2019s current research interests relate specifically to what affects the choices children and adolescents make about how they spend their time. Her work\u2014and that of her collaborators\u2014has identified two fundamental foundations of student choice: their belief that they can master a task, called _expectancy_ , and their belief that a task is worth doing, or _value_ .\n\n...\n\n2010. _The changing face(s) of race and gender in the United States._ Oxford : Wiley-Blackwell.  \nWatt, Helen M. Mentor-mentee pairs are encouraged to be receptive and eager to learn from one another, fostering a relationship grounded in mutual trust and open communication.\n\n* [Hui Xin Ng](mailto:hxng@ucsd.edu)\n* [Matin Yarmand](mailto:nsabri@ucsd.edu)\n* [Annapurna Vadaparty](mailto:avadaparty@ucsd.edu)\n* [Poorva Bedmutha](mailto:pbedmutha@ucsd.edu)\n\n## Reading group\n\nEvery two weeks, DISCO holds reading groups crafted to spark new discussions, share interdisciplinary perspectives, and gain inside knowledge. We aim to read papers with broad applications in design and human-computer interaction (HCI). Meetings begin with an informal presentation of the material led by the person who selected the week\u2019s paper. Members are encouraged to read the paper beforehand.\n\n* [Hayatpur](mailto:dshayatpur@ucsd.edu)\n\n## Coffee Roulette"}
{"timestamp": "2026-09-03T06:07:56.005634+00:00", "event": "identity_search_result", "candidate": "Annapurna Vadaparty", "field": "Math", "rank": 16, "query": "\"Annapurna Vadaparty\" site:ucsd.edu", "url": "https://apvadaparty.github.io/", "title": "Annapurna Vadaparty", "snippet": "Annapurna Vadaparty\n\n* Home\n* Research\n* Teaching\n\nBringing learning sciences to the classroom.\n\nAnnapurna Vadaparty\n\n# Annapurna Vadaparty\n\nPh.D. Candidate  \nComputing Education  \nUC San Diego\n\n* [Google Scholar](https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=L77bcNoAAAAJ&hl=en)\n* [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/in/annapurna-vadaparty/)\n\nHello!\n\nI'm a Ph.D. candidate in computing education at UC San Diego.\n\nBefore that, I studied [neuroscience](https://neuroscience.jhu.edu/) and [computer science](https://www.cs.jhu.edu/) at Johns Hopkins University,\nand I've always loved teaching. When I teach, I often find myself musing about what\nmakes something _click_ for a student\u2014what is it about a certain problem\nor explanation that changed their understanding?\n\nTo find out, I embarked on my graduate studies at UCSD that began in [Cognitive Science](https://cogsci.ucsd.edu/) and transitioned into Computing Education in the [Computer Science & Engineering](https://cse.ucsd.edu/) department. My\nresearch explores core competencies needed for computing in light of GenAI, and informs\nmy practices as an instructor and professional development facilitator. Goodwin\" \"Portland State University\"", "url": "https://www.pdx.edu/liberal-arts-sciences/inclusive-excellence", "title": "Inclusive Excellence | Portland State University", "snippet": "Search\n\nPortland State University - home\n\nCollege of Liberal Arts and Sciences\n\n# Inclusive Excellence\n\n## Fostering A Sense of Belonging\n\nPSU is committed to creating a campus climate where all students feel a sense of belonging and a commitment to their academic success. As part of our efforts to foster inclusive excellence, we are working to bring inclusive science pedagogy to scale through course-based undergraduate research experiences (CUREs), which make research more accessible by offering research opportunities to more students who otherwise would not have the opportunity.\n\nThrough focus groups in 2019, we learned that most PSU faculty _want_ to be more inclusive in their classrooms, _but don\u2019t know how_ to introduce inclusive and culturally-relevant pedagogy.\n\nOur pilot Inclusive Excellence CURE Fellows program, launched in Fall 2021 with funding from the [Howard Hughes Medical Institute](https://www.hhmi.\n\n...\n\n**Hunter Shobe** , Geography  \n**Ken Stedman** , Biology  \n**David Stuart** , Chemistry  \n**Eva Thanheiser** , Math + Stats  \n**Lisa Weasel** , Women, Gender & Sexuality Studies\n\n## [Portland State University](https://www.pdx.edu)\n\n### College of Liberal Arts and Sciences\n\nPortland State University  \n1721 SW Broadway, Suite 341  \nPortland OR 97201\n\nPhone:\n    [(503) 725-3514](tel:+15037253514)  \nEmail:\n    [clasofce@pdx.edu](mailto:clasofce@pdx.edu)\n\n* [](https://www.facebook.com/PortlandStateU/)\n* [PSU X profile icon](https://x.com/portland_state)\n* [](https://www.instagram.com/portlandstate/)\n* [](https://www.youtube.com/user/PortlandStateU/featured)\n* []"}

Shiyan Jiang\n\n# Shiyan Jiang\n\nAssociate Professor\n\nContact [jiang33@upenn.edu](mailto:jiang33@upenn.edu) [](tel:) Media Inquiries\n\nJump To\n\n* Biography\n* Journal Editorial Boards\n\nGo To\n\n* Biography\n* Publications\n* Journal Editorial Boards\n\n## Biography\n\nShiyan Jiang is a learning scientist and interdisciplinary researcher whose work lies at the intersection of artificial intelligence (AI), education, and identity. Her research focuses on two interconnected strands: supporting teachers from diverse disciplinary backgrounds to integrate AI across subjects and designing AI tools that meaningfully enhance teaching and learning. Across both strands, she explores how learners negotiate their sense of self in relation to disciplinary knowledge, digital tools\u2014especially AI\u2014and future possibilities."}

Matz\" site:umich.edu", "url": "https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/harnessing-the-power-of-research-to-elevate-education/", "title": "Harnessing the Power of Research to Elevate Education \u2013 Center for Academic Innovation", "snippet": "## Harnessing the Power of Research to Elevate Education\n\n* [May 21, 2025](https://ai.umich.edu/2025/05/21/)\n\n## This year\u2019s Research & Analytics Showcase gathered those working to improve the future of teaching and learning\n\nBecky Matz, research scientist lead at the Center for Academic Innovation, introduces the featured speakers at the center\u2019s Research & Analytics Showcase on April 30, 2025.\n\n**Mindy Arbaugh, Content Specialist**\n\nFrom studying the effectiveness of simulated medical procedures to building artificial intelligence agentic tools, there was no shortage of illuminating findings at the 2025 Research & Analytics Showcase, held on April 30 at the Center for Academic Innovation.\n\nThe event featured research from center staff, [Student Fellows](https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/impact-innovation-and-real-world-experience-highlighted-at-the-student-fellowship-showcase/) , and University of Michigan faculty and students.\n\n...\n\numich.edu/tag/research/)\n\n### Recent Posts\n\n[](https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/time-for-reinvention-universities-must-define-ais-role-in-education/)\n\n### Time for Reinvention: Universities Must Define AI\u2019s Role in Education\n\n[Read More](https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/time-for-reinvention-universities-must-define-ais-role-in-education/)\n\n[](https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/marcus-collins-encourages-students-to-embrace-the-unknown-explore-passions/)\n\n### Marcus Collins Encourages Students to Embrace the Unknown, Explore Passions\n\n[Read More](https://ai.umich.edu/blog-posts/marcus-collins-encourages-students-to-embrace-the-unknown-explore-passions"}

{"timestamp": "2026-09-03T06:09:22.283693+00:00", "event": "identity_search_result", "candidate": "Katherine Muenks", "field": "Math", "rank": 6, "query": "\"Katherine Muenks\" \"The University of Texas at Austin\"", "url": "https://sites.edb.utexas.edu/lamb/people/", "title": "Lab Members - Learning and Motivational Beliefs Lab", "snippet": "# Lab Members\n\n### Principal Investigator\n\n* * *\n\n**Katherine \u201cKatie\u201d Muenks, Ph.D.** , is an associate professor in the Department of Educational Psychology and the principal investigator of the Learning and Motivational Beliefs (LAMB) Lab at the University of Texas at Austin. Her research focuses on what motivates students to succeed and how teachers, parents, and peers create motivationally supportive or unsupportive environments. She is particularly interested in studying contexts in which students experience difficulty and/or failure, and has done most of her work in the fields of Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics (STEM). She is also interested in how to create equitable learning environments that reduce achievement and attainment gaps based on race/ethnicity, gender, and socioeconomic status.\n\nKatie received her B.S. in Psychology from Ohio State University and her Ph.D. at the University of Maryland. She completed a postdoctoral fellowship at Indiana University."}

Rosenzweig\" \"University of Georgia\"", "url": "https://motivatelab.org/emily-rosenzweig", "title": "Emily Rosenzweig \u2014 Motivate lab", "snippet": "# Emily Rosenzweig\n\nGood+Professional+One.png\n\n## **Emily Rosenzweig**\n\n### Consultant\n\n**Emily Rosenzweig** is an assistant professor in the Department of Educational Psychology at the University of Georgia. Her research focuses on understanding what drives students to learn and reach their academic potential. Her research lies at the intersection of educational, developmental, and social psychology and explores how to understand and improve students' motivation during middle school, high school, and college. [All Posts](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/posts)\n2. [News](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/category/news/)\n3. [Pharmaceutics News](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/category/news/pharmaceutics_news/)\n4. Dr. Fan Zhang named UF Health Cancer Center Rising Star of the Year\n\n# Dr. Fan Zhang named UF Health Cancer Center Rising Star of the Year\n\nPublished On January 30, 2025\n\nSocial Media\n\nShare this article\n\n[By Tyler Francischine](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/author/tyler413/)\n\n[Fan Zhang, Ph.D.,](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/profile/zhang-fan-10/) an assistant professor of pharmaceutics in the University of Florida College of Pharmacy, is a 2025 recipient of the UF Health Cancer Center Rising Star of the Year Award.\n\nThe award, which recognizes early-stage investigators whose accomplishments distinguish them as possible future leaders in the Cancer Center, was bestowed upon Zhang during a [Jan. 10 ceremony](https://cancer.ufl."}

Dr. Fan Zhang receives NIH grant to study interactions of myeloid cells with nanotherapeutics\n\n# Dr. Fan Zhang receives NIH grant to study interactions of myeloid cells with nanotherapeutics\n\nPublished On August 26, 2024\n\nSocial Media\n\nShare this article\n\n[By Tyler Francischine](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/author/tyler413/)\n\n[Fan Zhang, Ph.D.](https://pharmacy.ufl.edu/profile/zhang-fan-10/) , an assistant professor of pharmaceutics in the University of Florida College of Pharmacy, has received a $1.9 million grant from the National Institutes of Health\u2019s National Institute of General Medical Sciences to study the role of myeloid cells \u2013 critical mediators of inflammation \u2013 in nanotherapeutic treatments of diseases like cancer, sepsis, and autoimmune disorders.\n\nMyeloid cells such as inflammatory monocytes are a type of immune cell originating in bone marrow."}

{"timestamp": "2026-09-03T06:09:47.585660+00:00", "event": "identity_search_result", "candidate": "Wanli Xing", "field": "Math", "rank": 7, "query": "\"Wanli Xing\" \"University of Florida\"", "url": "https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/0144929X.2021.1936176", "title": "Understanding students\u2019 effective use of data in the age of big data in higher education: Behaviour & Information Technology: Vol 41, No 12", "snippet": "We used an online questionnaire that collected 242 completed surveys from Chinese university students. Partial Least Squares (PLS) path modelling was used to analyse the data. The initial findings support the conclusion that university students could be encouraged to effectively use data in three ways: (1) through the promotion of university-wide cultures of data use and sustained improvements in data quality, (2) through the professional development of student data literacy, and (3) through the support of student data autonomy, student data reflectiveness, and students' digital identities.\n\nKEYWORDS:\n\n* Use of data\n* data-driven decision\n* e-learning\n* ease of use perceptions and behavioural intentions\n\n## Disclosure statement\n\nNo potential conflict of interest was reported by the author(s).\n\n## Additional information\n\n### Notes on contributors\n\n#### Wanli Xing _**Wanli Xing**_ is an Assistant Professor of Educational Technology at the University of Florida."}

Elias\" \"Rutgers University\"", "url": "http://psych.rutgers.edu/people/chair-v-cs/faculty-profile/93-maurice-elias", "title": "Elias, Maurice", "snippet": "Areas: Clinical Psychology **Co-Director, Academy for SEL; Professor of Psychology, Rutgers; Director, Rutgers Social-Emotional Learning Lab; Director, Collaborative Center for Community-Based Research and Service** Maurice Elias is Professor, Psychology Department, Rutgers University, Director of the Rutgers Social-Emotional Learning Lab, and Academic Director of The Collaborative, Rutgers' Center for Community-Based Research and Service. He is Past President of the Society for Community Research and Action/Division of Community Psychology (27) of APA and has received the SCRA Distinguished Contribution to Practice and Ethnic Minority Mentoring Awards, as well as APA's National Psychological Consultants to Management Award, the Joseph E. Zins Memorial Senior Scholar Award for Social-Emotional Learning from the Collaborative for Academic, Social, and Emotional Learning (CASEL), the John P. McGovern Medal from the American School Health Association, and the Sanford McDonnell Award for Lifetime Achievement in Character Education. Dr. Elias lectures nationally and internationally to educators and parents about students' emotional intelligence, school success, and social-emotional and character development."}

Elias is a professor in the Psychology Department at Rutgers University, director of the Rutgers [Social-Emotional and Character Development Lab](https://www.secdlab.org) , and co-director of the [Academy for Social-Emotional Learning in Schools](https://SELinSchools.org) , which offers certificate programs in direct instruction and school leadership relating to SEL and character development for educators and student support professionals in school and out-of-school settings. He has received the Sanford McDonnell Award for Lifetime Achievement in Character Education and the Joseph E.
